# Dry-run CUJ — UI drives the tool on a bucket video → reel downloaded local (multilingual)

> **Status:** `DRAFT` — owner-gated · **Owner:** Avi Dullu · **Created:** 2026-06-21 · **Goal for this session.**
> **Umbrella:** a milestone of `docs/LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` (the appliance) + `docs/PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md` (the shipped SPA back-half).
> **Honesty note:** grounded in a file:line integration-surface map (this session); claims tagged `[verified]` / `[gap]`.

## 0. The goal (owner's words)
Operate the **web UI** → point at a **video on the GCS bucket** → the engine (tool) processes it → **review/select rallies in the UI** → **compile** → **download the reel to the local machine**. Then **repeat with the UI in Hindi** to prove the multilingual workflow.

## 1. What already works vs the gap `[verified]`
**✅ Back half (shipped — PER_RALLY_SELECTION M0):** the SPA loads `?video=<id>` → `POST /api/query` (rallies, default all-selected) → deterministic query/select → `POST /api/compile` → `<video>` preview + **`<a download>` saves the MP4 locally**. Reel download-to-local **works today** (`GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename}` streams it; `/api/compile` also returns `download_url`). Fully **en/kn/hi** (the switcher / `?lang=`).

**⛔ Front half — the gap is the SPA, NOT the engine `[corrected 2026-06-21; see §8]`:**
- The SPA has **no ingest/process UI** — it can't point at a video (bucket or local), can't call `/api/process`, can't poll `/api/jobs`. It only gets a `video_id` from `?video=` in the URL. **This SPA front-half is the *only* net-new work for Path B (= B-P2).**
- **HTTP bucket-ingest ALREADY EXISTS in the engine (shipped M2 / [#280]) `[verified file:line]`:** `POST /api/process` accepts a `gs://`/`gcs://` (also `s3://`/`local://`) `video_path` directly. Submit resolves the URI and **skips the local-existence check for non-local backends** (`backend/main.py:274,283` → `storage.resolve_input_ref`; `gs://`→`gcs` normalized in `backend/storage/ref.py:60`); the job worker then fetches it to scratch (`_execute_processing` → `storage.acquire_local_input`, `main.py:159`) and runs the **same** hash → validate → segment → DB pipeline as a local file — writing a queryable `video_id` into the API's SQLite `videos`/`play_segments`. Proven end-to-end by `tests/test_api_endpoints.py::test_process_nonlocal_uri_skips_exists_and_fetches` (gs:// → 202, not 404 → job `done` → `db.get_video(video_id)` row with the **source URI preserved**), plus the hosted-mode-rejection / unknown-scheme-400 / CLI-variant tests.
  - *Where the earlier "net-new" claim went wrong:* it conflated the **API path** (`/api/process`, which DID gain URI ingest in M2) with the separate **worker CLI** (`python -m backend.worker infer --video-uri gcs://…`), which writes `intervals.csv`/`report.json` to the bucket — **not** the API DB. The CLI distinction is real; the "the endpoint is local-only" conclusion was not. ⚠ **Engine-surface claims drift between sessions** (the engine ships PRs independently) — re-verify any `[gap]`/`net-new` engine claim against the cited `file:line` before scoping it as work.

## 2. Two paths to the goal
**Path A — Cheapest dry run NOW (~zero new code).** Pre-process the bucket video out-of-band to mint a `video_id` in the engine DB, then drive the existing SPA back-half. Proves the **multilingual UI-driven workflow** end-to-end. The only non-UI step is "point at the bucket" (a one-line CLI pre-process).

**Path B — Full "point at a bucket video IN the UI" (SPA-only — smaller than first scoped).** The **HTTP bucket-ingest endpoint already exists** (§1) — so Path B is **only the SPA front-half** (appliance S1/S2): an **ingest** input (enter a `gs://` URI) → `POST /api/process` → an **ingest + progress** screen polling `GET /api/jobs/{id}` → on `done`, load `result.video_id` (which the existing back-half already renders). **No net-new engine endpoint** — B-P1 is already done; the remaining work ...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

Memory corrected. Here's what the recon established, and my recommendation.

## The reframe (this changes the decision)

**B-P2 is already done.** Your icebreaker framed the alpha-shareable path as *"the khelsutra `web/` B-P2 ingest screen + the M7 Cloud Run service deploy."* But the SPA ingest/progress screen shipped in khelsutra [#56](https://github.com/Khelsutra/khelsutra/pull/56) (`IngestPanel.tsx` + `useIngestJob.ts` + tests) and was **proven live e2e** — in-UI `gs://` → `/api/process` → poll `/api/jobs` → 12 rallies → reel — per [DRY_RUN_CUJ.md §9](../khelsutra/docs/DRY_RUN_CUJ.md) and appliance tracker **T12 ☑**. The handoff + gap-memory were stale (now fixed).

So **alpha-shareable is now a deploy/auth task, not an SPA-build task.** Here's the file:line-verified gap:

| Piece | State | Who |
|---|---|---|
| SPA ingest→reel flow (B-P2) | ✅ shipped + live-verified (#56) | done |
| CORS | ✅ already env-driven (`RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS`, [main.py:48-68](backend/main.py:48)) | done |
| Edge-auth (M9) | ✅ code merged, default-OFF ([auth.py](backend/api/auth.py)) | done |
| Engine `0.0.0.0`+`PORT` bind | ❌ hard-bound `127.0.0.1`, no `PORT` ([main.py:756](backend/main.py:756)) | **Claude (opt-in)** |
| FastAPI **service** Dockerfile + deploy script | ❌ `deploy/cloudrun/` builds only the GPU **Job** worker | **Claude** |
| SPA `VITE_API_BASE` build env | ❌ `/api` hardcoded same-origin ([client.ts:7](../khelsutra/web/src/api/client.ts:7)) | **Claude** |
| Exposure-safety (enforce `allowed_video_root` + posture test) | partial | **Claude** |
| Stand up box / `gcloud run deploy`; flip edge-auth + CF team/AUD/ingress; rotate Gemini key | ❌ | ⛔ **owner** |

**Two viable topologies — and which one you pick decides whether there's any meaningful Claude *code* on the alpha path right now:**
- **(i) cloudflared Tunnel + CF Access in front of the existing engine+SPA on one box** — the appliance design (D1/D2), **$0 standing**, SQLite single-writer trivially satisfied, and the droplet `168.144.126.176` *already runs engine+site* (Gemini works on its CPU). This is the **cheapest, fastest** path — it's ~90% your ops (tunnel + CF Access + key rotation) and **almost no new engine code**. To make your existing local CUJ shareable, this is the shortest line.
- **(ii) Cloud Run managed service** — "serverless, no box to babysit," but needs the three Claude code slices above **+ ~$18–25/mo standing** (LB) and `max-instances=1` to protect the single-writer SQLite.

**On the engine code alternatives:** **M12 gdrive-api** is clean Claude-doable-no-spend (~300 LOC mirroring `gcs.py` DI + fake, **no DB migration**) — but it's a storage backend, *not* the UI fix. **M11 flywheel** is privacy-sensitive: only a default-OFF *skeleton* is doable here; the real consent schema/UI/DPA is counsel-gated and it depends on M9's not-yet-landed v7 consent migration. So M11 deserves fresh focus *later*, not now.

## My recommendation

**Pursue the alpha-shareable path first — via topology (i), the Tunnel-to-a-box.** It's the shortest line to "a page I can share," costs ~$0, and matches the design you already wrote. Because it's mostly your ops, the best **Claude code** to land alongside it is the **turnkey + safety glue**: the exposure-safety posture (enforce `allowed_video_root`, a test that raw `:8000` is refused from outside) + cloudflared/reverse-proxy config templates + a deploy runbook — all isolated, default-OFF, no spend. That makes your tunnel deploy safe and ~3 commands.

If you'd rather go serverless (ii), I'll instead land the three code slices (opt-in PORT/0.0.0.0 → service Dockerfile + deploy script → SPA `VITE_API_BASE`) as isolated PRs, then hand you the `gcloud` + CF steps.

Let me lock the direction with you:

9	    bucket can never serve). This is the README §4.3 "loud error before any job".
10	  * a **GPU mismatch** (e.g. ``device='cuda'`` on a box where CUDA isn't visible, or ``device='auto'``
11	    falling back to CPU) → a **warning**, never an error: the probe is best-effort (torch may live
12	    only in the detector subprocess env — see ``presets.probe_cuda_available``), and the detector's
13	    own healthcheck is the real authority. A warning surfaces it early without false-failing.
14	  * **creds that can't be confirmed** (no ``GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`` / not on Cloud Run) →

16	    we can't probe offline, so the storage backend's own init is the authority; we just flag it.

1	"""Cloudflare Access edge-auth — verify the Cf-Access JWT, derive the tenant ``user_id`` (M9, D9).
2	
3	**DEFAULT-OFF:** when ``security.edge_auth_enabled`` is False the resolver is a strict NO-OP (returns
4	``None`` = the local single-user) — nothing about the existing local path changes. When enabled, the
5	``Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion`` header is **required** and **verified** (RS256) against the team JWKS —
6	signature + exp + issuer + audience — so a direct hit to the Cloud Run URL **can't spoof** the
7	identity header (the explicit D9 anti-spoof requirement). Returns the verified ``user_id`` (the
8	token's ``email``, else ``sub``).
9	
10	``jwt`` (``PyJWT[crypto]``) is **lazy-imported** so the base runtime works without it (install the
11	``auth`` extra for hosted/multi-tenant). The JWKS fetch is **injectable**, so the whole verifier is
12	unit-tested **offline** with an in-test RSA keypair + a fake JWKS — no network, no Cloudflare.
13	"""
14	
15	from __future__ import annotations
16	
17	import json
18	import time
19	import urllib.request
20	from typing import Any, Callable, Mapping, Optional
21	
22	# Cloudflare Access sends the signed assertion in this header (it terminates at the CF edge).
23	CF_ACCESS_HEADER = "Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion"
24	_JWKS_PATH = "/cdn-cgi/access/certs"
25	_JWKS_TTL_SEC = 600.0  # re-fetch the team JWKS at most every 10 min (keys rotate slowly)
26	
27	JwksFetcher = Callable[[str], dict]
28	
29	
30	class EdgeAuthError(Exception):
31	    """A required Cf-Access token was missing or failed verification → the caller returns 401/403."""
32	
33	
34	# module-level JWKS cache: team_domain -> (fetched_at, jwks_dict). Production only; tests inject.
35	_jwks_cache: dict[str, tuple[float, dict]] = {}
36	
37	
38	def _issuer(team_domain: str) -> str:
39	    return team_domain.rstrip("/")
40	
41	
42	def _default_jwks_fetcher(team_domain: str) -> dict:
43	    """Fetch the team's public JWKS (``<team_domain>/cdn-cgi/access/certs``). Network — only the
44	    production path reaches here; tests pass an injected fetcher."""
45	    url = _issuer(team_domain) + _JWKS_PATH
46	    with urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=5) as resp:  # noqa: S310 (https team domain)
47	        data: Any = json.load(resp)
48	    if not isinstance(data, dict) or "keys" not in data:
49	        raise EdgeAuthError(f"unexpected JWKS shape from {url}")
50	    return data
51	
52	
53	def _get_jwks(team_domain: str, fetcher: Optional[JwksFetcher]) -> dict:
54	    """Return the JWKS for ``team_domain``, cached with a TTL. An injected ``fetcher`` (tests)
55	    bypasses the cache so each test is deterministic."""
56	    if fetcher is not None:
57	        return fetcher(team_domain)
58	    now = time.time()
59	    cached = _jwks_cache.get(team_domain)
60	    if cached and (now - cached[0]) < _JWKS_TTL_SEC:
61	        return cached[1]
62	    jwks = _default_jwks_fetcher(team_domain)
63	    _jwks_cache[team_domain] = (now, jwks)
64	    return jwks
65	
66

47	
48	def _cors_origins_from_env(env: Optional[Dict[str, str]] = None) -> List[str]:
49	    """M9: CORS allow-origins from the ``RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS`` env var (comma-separated),
50	    default ``["*"]``. Read from the ENV (not a cwd ``config.json``) so importing ``backend.main``
51	    does zero filesystem I/O — a hosted/multi-tenant deploy sets this to lock CORS to its origin."""
52	    src = os.environ if env is None else env
53	    raw = (src.get("RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS", "") or "").strip()
54	    origins = [o.strip() for o in raw.split(",") if o.strip()]
55	    return origins or ["*"]
56	
57	
58	# CORS for the web UI. allow_origins='*' with allow_credentials=True is rejected by browsers per the
59	# fetch spec + is unsafe to carry into multi-tenant; this tool uses no cookies, so credentials stay
60	# off. The origin list is configurable via the env var above (default ["*"]) so a hosted deploy can
61	# lock it to its origin; the middleware is fixed at startup.
62	app.add_middleware(
63	    CORSMiddleware,
64	    allow_origins=_cors_origins_from_env(),
65	    allow_credentials=False,
66	    allow_methods=["*"],
67	    allow_headers=["*"],
68	)
69	
70	# Serves static frontend files directly (now under api/ per approved structure)
71	os.makedirs("backend/api/static", exist_ok=True)

### 77. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.712Z)

The test conventions are clear. My PR-1 design (tight, default-OFF, server-scoped):

- Add an **exposure-safety posture** to `startup.py`, gated on `security.edge_auth_enabled` (default `False` → strict no-op, byte-identical to today). When the owner flips it on to expose the engine, enforce at **boot** (fail-fast, not per-request):
  - `EXPOSED_NO_VIDEO_ROOT` (**ERROR**) — `allowed_video_root` unset → an authenticated tenant could make `/api/process` read an arbitrary local file.
  - `EXPOSED_EDGE_AUTH_INCOMPLETE` (**ERROR**) — `cf_team_domain`/`cf_access_aud` missing → lifts the current first-request failure to boot.
  - `EXPOSED_AUTH_EXTRA_MISSING` (**WARN**) — `PyJWT[crypto]` not installed → would 500 every request.
- **Server-scoped** via an `include_exposure` flag (worker stays byte-identical — it reads `--video-uri`, not untrusted tenant paths).

and at request time (folded from the adversarial review wf_35b735a9, which confirmed default-OFF

### 188. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.719Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Open the PR
command: cd /c/Users/avidu/Projects/badminton-highlight-indexer
gh pr create --base master --head serving/exposure-safety-preflight --title "feat(serving): exposure-safety boot posture — fail fast on a half-configured exposed engine (default-OFF)" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## What & why

**M9 hardening for the alpha-shareable "tunnel-to-a-box" topology** (owner's top priority: a UI-driven, shareable page — engine+SPA behind a Cloudflare-Access gate). When the owner exposes the engine (flips `security.edge_auth_enabled` on), the edge gate is only *half* the safety story: today nothing at boot enforces the rest of the posture, so a half-configured exposure starts *looking* healthy then leaks / 500s per request.

This adds an **exposure-safety posture** that fails fast at **boot** (server only), so the engine refuses to start unsafe instead of discovering it per-request.

## The checks (in `backend/config/startup.py`, gated on `security.edge_auth_enabled`)
| Code | Level | Fires when | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `EXPOSED_NO_VIDEO_ROOT` | **fatal** | `allowed_video_root` jail unset/blank | an authenticated tenant could make `/api/process` read an arbitrary local file |
| `EXPOSED_EDGE_AUTH_INCOMPLETE` | **fatal** | `cf_team_domain`/`cf_access_aud` missing | the Cf-Access JWT can't verify → lifts the current *first-request* 401 to boot |
| `EXPOSED_AUTH_EXTRA_MISSING` | **warn** | `PyJWT[crypto]` not importable | would 500 every request; heads-up, not fatal |

## Default-OFF (no behaviour change for today's deployments)
- `edge_auth_enabled=False` (today's default) → the exposure checks are a **strict no-op**.
- Server-only: wired via a new `include_exposure=True` from `_enforce_startup_or_exit`. The **worker never opts in** (it reads `--video-uri`, not untrusted tenant paths), so it's unaffected even with a shared config.
- The `run_startup_checks` restructure keeps the M5 per-profile path byte-identical.

## Adversarial review (`wf_35b735a9`, 3 lenses)
Confirmed **default-OFF byte-identical** + **no bypass** (the `resolve_under_root` jail is realpath/commonpath-based, traversal-safe; `_enforce_startup_or_exit` only runs on the server-start path, never the CLI). Folded:
- a **whitespace drift** — aligned `auth.resolve_tenant` to `.strip()` team/AUD so "configured" means the same at boot and at request time;
- named the config fields in the edge-auth-incomplete message;
- added the WARN-only-doesn't-exit test.

**Deferred to the PR-2 deploy-kit runbook** (out of scope here): document that `upload_dir` must sit under `allowed_video_root`, and that you MUST set `edge_auth_enabled=True` when exposing (these checks only fire then).

## Tests / gates
- +15 startup tests + a whitespace edge-auth test (default-OFF no-op, worker-ignores, both-fatals-compose-with-profile-checks, server-exit, WARN-only-no-exit).
- `python run_all_tests.py` → **1259 passed** (rebased on `origin/master` incl. #293). `ruff` + `mypy` clean. Coverage 85% (floor 70%).

Tracker + changelog updated in `docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/README.md` (M9 row + changelog).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -5
[/external_agent_tool_call]

--- head of provision-vm.sh ---
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# First-class, PROVIDER-AGNOSTIC provisioning for the KhelSutra site (Node + SQLite host).
# Run from your LOCAL machine against a FRESH Ubuntu VM (DigitalOcean / GCE / any cloud):
# it pushes site/ via `git archive` (PRIVATE-REPO-SAFE — no clone, token, or secret on the
# VM), installs Node, installs + starts a systemd service, opens the firewall, and VERIFIES
# it is actually serving. Idempotent (safe to re-run). Pairs with teardown-vm.sh so a
# destroy → resume-on-a-different-provider cycle is clean and reproducible.
#
#   SSH_TARGET=root@1.2.3.4 [SSH_KEY=~/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet] \
#   [REMOTE_DIR=/srv/khelsutra-site] [PORT=8787] [NODE_MAJOR=24] [RESTORE_DB=./backup.db] \
#   site/scripts/provision-vm.sh
#
# NODE_MAJOR must be >= 24 so `node:sqlite` is unflagged (server.js imports it directly).
set -euo pipefail
: "${SSH_TARGET:?set SSH_TARGET=user@host}"
KEY="${SSH_KEY:-$HOME/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet}"
DIR="${REMOTE_DIR:-/srv/khelsutra-site}"
PORT="${PORT:-8787}"
NODE_MAJOR="${NODE_MAJOR:-24}"
SITE="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"          # the site/ dir (script lives in site/scripts/)
REPO="$(cd "$SITE/.." && pwd)"                      # repo root (for git archive)
SSHO=(-i "$KEY" -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new -o ConnectTimeout=10)

echo "→ [1/5] waiting for SSH at $SSH_TARGET ..."
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do ssh "${SSHO[@]}" "$SSH_TARGET" true 2>/dev/null && break || sleep 5; done
ssh "${SSHO[@]}" "$SSH_TARGET" true                 # final attempt — fail loud if still down

echo "→ [2/5] pushing site/ via git archive (private-repo-safe; no secret on the VM) ..."
ssh "${SSHO[@]}" "$SSH_TARGET" "rm -rf '$DIR/site' && mkdir -p '$DIR/site'"
# core.autocrlf=false forces LF in the archive even when the operator runs on Windows —
# autocrlf=true otherwise overrides .gitattributes eol=lf, producing CRLF on the VM and a
# byte-for-byte parity drift vs Cloudflare (which serves the canonical LF blobs).
git -C "$REPO" -c core.autocrlf=false archive HEAD:site | ssh "${SSHO[@]}" "$SSH_TARGET" "tar -x -C '$DIR/site'"

if [ -n "${RESTORE_DB:-}" ]; then
[/external_agent_tool_result]

=== engine HOSTING_PLAN.md (edge-gate canonical design) ===
# Hosting Plan — khelsutra.guru

**Status:** PROPOSAL — awaiting owner ratification.
**Date:** 2026-06-10 · **Constraints:** budget $10–25/mo · GCP credits available · DigitalOcean
acceptable · alpha backend = owner's GPU box behind a tunnel · 10–25 alpha testers · registrar = GoDaddy.

> **⚠ Update (2026-06-20, ADR-013): DigitalOcean is DROPPED for COMPUTE** — GCP is the sole compute
> stack (training + serving). The **"All-DigitalOcean"** compute option below is retired; DO Spaces
> remains only a *possible* S3-compatible storage target. Edge/site hosting choices here are unaffected.
**Companion:** `docs/UI_PROPOSAL.md`.

## 1. Recommendation in one line

**Alpha: free edge + your box** (Cloudflare DNS/Pages/Access/Tunnel → FastAPI on the GPU box) at
**$0/mo**, saving GCP credits; **Beta: GCP control plane + your box as pull-worker** (Cloud Run +
GCS + Firebase Auth, credits-covered), keeping the edge where it's free.

Rationale for the Cloudflare pieces despite the GCP preference: GCP has no free equivalent of the
Tunnel + Access combo (IAP requires a load balancer, ~$18/mo standing cost), and the alpha needs
exactly three things GCP charges for but Cloudflare gives free: a secure tunnel to a home box with
a custom domain, email-allowlist auth (free ≤50 users — fits 10–25 testers), and static hosting.
Credits are better spent at beta on compute/storage, which is where GCP is excellent.

## 2. Alpha topology ($0/mo)

```
tester browser
   │  https
   ▼
Cloudflare edge (free): DNS + TLS + Access (email-OTP allowlist)
   ├── app.khelsutra.guru  → Cloudflare Pages (React SPA; auto-deploy from GitHub)
   └── api.khelsutra.guru  → Cloudflare Tunnel (outbound-only `cloudflared` service on the box)
                                  │
                                  ▼
                       FastAPI :8000 on the GPU box (Windows)
                       — Gemini segmenter path (no GPU in request path)
                       — async jobs (#16), SQLite WAL, output/ on disk
```

- **No inbound ports opened** on the home network; `cloudflared` dials out. Home IP never exposed.
- **Auth:** Cloudflare Access in front of BOTH hostnames; backend trusts the
  `Cf-Access-Authenticated-User-Email` header (verify the Access JWT for rigor) for per-user scoping.
- **Upload limit:** free-tier proxy caps request bodies at **100 MB** → SPA uploads in ≤90 MB
  chunks (UI_PROPOSAL B2). Match videos (1–4 GB) flow fine as chunks.
- **Box offline = service offline.** Acceptable for alpha; SPA shows a friendly "processing engine
  offline" banner (a `/api/health` ping). Beta removes this with the queue.
- **Costs:** Pages, Tunnel, Access, DNS = $0. Only the Gemini per-run cost (~$0.05/match) and
  domain renewal.

## 3. Beta topology (GCP credits; ~$15–20/mo post-credits)

```
browser ──► Cloudflare edge (free, unchanged) ──► app.khelsutra.guru (Pages)
                                              └─► api.khelsutra.guru ──► Cloud Run (FastAPI control plane, scale-to-zero)
                                                                             │ jobs table (Cloud SQL Postgres)
                                                                             │ signed resumable URLs
                                                                             ▼
                                                                        GCS buckets (uploads / reels, lifecycle → cold)
                                                                             ▲ pull jobs, outbound-only
                                                                        GPU box = first `byo_worker` (PLATFORM P3)
```

- Uploads go **browser → GCS directly** (resumable signed URLs) — no proxy body limit, no home
  bandwidth for ingest; the box pulls only what it processes (WASB/owned-model jobs), Cloud Run
  handles Gemini-path jobs entirely in-cloud.
- Auth graduates to **Firebase Auth** (free; Google + email sign-in...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

43	    });

## Exactly what I need from counsel (for M11 live + M9-consent)

Hand counsel this. **Context:** free users get highlight reels; in exchange we'd retain their uploaded footage + derived data (trajectories/labels) to train *our own* model. Paid tier = no training on their data (privacy is the paid feature). We're India-based (**DPDP Act**) and may serve EU users (**GDPR**). Gemini sees footage at *inference only*, never as a training source (a hard product guardrail).

1. **ToS / consent text** for the free-tier "data-for-reels" trade — the exact wording a user agrees to: what data, what use, retention, how to withdraw.
2. **Consent granularity** — is one "I consent to training use" attestation enough, or do you need *separate* consent for raw footage vs derived data?
3. **Minors** — we train **adults-only** (minor footage → reel yes, training **no**). Should we require verifiable parental-consent capture for any minor footage, or just **hard-block minor footage from the training pool** entirely (simpler/safer)? Your call decides the schema.
4. **Retention** — confirm the plan: train on **derived** data + **auto-delete raw** footage; raw kept *only* with explicit consent for the training pool. What's the max retention window + the retention classes?
5. **Right-to-erasure** (DPDP/GDPR) — do we need a per-user data-deletion hook? (If yes, the schema needs a stable consent/user id.)
6. **Default posture** I'll hardcode (please confirm): *no consent attested ⇒ no training, raw auto-deleted.*
7. **DPA / disclosure** — confirm the ToS discloses the Gemini upload (inference sub-processor).

I'll encode whatever you decide; until then M11 ships as an **inert, default-OFF skeleton** (no data captured, no behavior change).

def for_video(root: str, video_id: str, prefix: str = "workspaces") -> VideoWorkspace:

class BillingConfig(BaseModel):
    """Per-job cost ledger (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M8, D8).

    **DEFAULT-OFF:** ``enabled=False`` → nothing records cost (the v6 cost columns stay NULL =
    today's behaviour). When enabled, a job's cost is computed + stored in **USD** via the versioned
    ``pricebook`` DB table and **displayed in INR** at ``fx_inr_per_usd``. The default pricebook
    version is the seeded ``placeholder-v0`` (every rate ``0.0`` → cost ``0``) until the owner
    inserts a real, calibrated version and points ``pricebook_version`` at it.

    ⚠ Even with ``enabled=True`` + a real pricebook, recorded cost stays **0** until a follow-up
    wires the worker to emit per-job ``usage`` (gpu/wall/egress/gemini) — M8 lands the ledger +
    pricebook + the recording seam; usage emission (the metering hooks) is a separate step."""

    enabled: bool = False
    pricebook_version: str = "placeholder-v0"   # the seeded $0 version; owner flips to a real one
    fx_inr_per_usd: float = 83.0                  # USD→INR display rate (Bangalore market)
    margin: float = 0.0                            # resale markup (≥0); 0 = bill at cost


class AppConfig(BaseModel):

def _drain_due_jobs() -> int:
    """One worker tick: claim and run every currently-runnable job (queued, or waiting whose
    `next_poll_at` is due), earliest-due first, until the table has nothing runnable.

    Each parked job (`JobWaiting` → status 'waiting') drops out of this drain and is picked
    up by a FUTURE drain once due; we schedule that follow-up drain via `_schedule_drain` so
    a single-worker box keeps making progress with no central timer (the DB is the clock).
    Returns the number of jobs that reached a terminal/parked state this tick.
    """
    import backend.main as _main
    ran = 0
    while True:
        job = _main.db_instance.claim_due_job()
        if job is None:
            break
        ran += 1
        _main._run_claimed_job(job)
    # If anything is parked-but-not-yet-due, arrange a wake-up so it resumes without a new
    # submit. On the single-box executor this is a delayed re-drain; multi-worker just relies
=== job_worker imports/top ===
"""Async job worker — drain/wake loop (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16, EXECUTION_POLICY.md P3).

Extracted verbatim from backend.main to shrink the orchestrator; behavior is unchanged.
The worker DRAINS the jobs table via `claim_due_job` and honours WAIT_AND_RESUME: a job
that raises `JobWaiting` is parked (status 'waiting' + `next_poll_at` + progress) and
re-claimed when due — with no central scheduler (the DB is the clock).

The monkeypatch contract these functions rely on (and that tests depend on):
every cross-function seam — `db_instance`, `global_config`, `_execute_processing`,
`_arm_wake_timer`, `_get_executor`, `_MAX_DRAIN_WAKE_SEC` — is resolved THROUGH the
`backend.main` module object at call-time (`import backend.main as _main`), never as a
bare local. `backend.main` re-exports these names, so `monkeypatch.setattr(backend.main,
...)` still intercepts the inter-function calls (e.g. a parked job arms the *patched*
`_arm_wake_timer`, so no real daemon timer leaks in tests). Importing `backend.main` lazily
inside the functions also avoids the circular import at module load (main.py imports this
module; this module must not import main at import time).
"""
import logging
import threading
import traceback
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
from typing import Any, Dict, Optional

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# Async job executor (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16). max_workers=1 on purpose: a single
# self-hosted box serializes GPU work, and the Gemini provider is rate-fragile under
# parallel uploads (2026-06-10 ops learning: serial uploads + backoff). The segmenters
# already parallelize their AI handoff internally via their own pools.
_job_executor: Optional[ThreadPoolExecutor] = None

101	def _maybe_record_job_cost(job: Dict[str, Any], result: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
102	    """M8 cost ledger (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M8, D8). DEFAULT-OFF: a no-op unless
103	    ``config.billing.enabled``. When on, compute the job's cost from ``result['usage']``
104	    (= {gpu_seconds, wall_seconds, egress_gb, gemini_usd, gpu_type}) × the versioned pricebook, and
105	    persist it.
106	
107	    ⚠ **Honest gap (M8 scope):** the pipeline does **not** emit ``result['usage']`` yet, so even with
108	    ``billing.enabled=True`` AND a real calibrated pricebook, every recorded cost is **0** until a
109	    FOLLOW-UP wires the worker to emit usage (gpu/wall/egress/gemini). M8 lands the ledger + pricebook
110	    + the recording seam; the usage-emission step is separate (the metering hooks on the real run).
111	    With the placeholder pricebook the cost is 0 regardless.
112	
113	    Best-effort — never fails a completed job because cost bookkeeping hiccuped. ``job`` is the
114	    claimed row (carries ``owner_id``); ``result`` is the pipeline output."""
115	    import backend.main as _main
116	
117	    billing_cfg = getattr(_main.global_config, "billing", None)
118	    if not billing_cfg or not getattr(billing_cfg, "enabled", False):
119	        return
120	    try:
121	        usage = (result or {}).get("usage") or {}
122	        _main.db_instance.record_job_cost(
123	            job["id"], usage=usage, pricebook_version=billing_cfg.pricebook_version,
124	            gpu_type=usage.get("gpu_type"), compute_backend=(result or {}).get("compute_backend"),
125	            owner_id=job.get("owner_id"), margin=billing_cfg.margin,
126	        )
127	        logger.info("Recorded cost for job %s (pricebook=%s).", job["id"], billing_cfg.pricebook_version)
128	    except Exception as e:  # never wedge a completed job on bookkeeping
129	        logger.warning("Cost recording for job %s failed (non-fatal): %s", job.get("id"), e)
130

opt-in, derived-data + auto-delete-raw, minors excluded.

day consent + the owned model + golden labels are wired. NO DB migration (the future M9-consent
migration is v8 — v7 was taken by the `locale` column). +14 tests incl. a guardrail-LOCK test that

## What & why
The **M11 data-flywheel harness** (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING, D12) — the consent→capture→eval→goldenize loop, landed as **inert plumbing**. When activated it captures a *consented adult* job's footage + rally output for later labeling → the golden training set, shadow-evals the owned model vs Gemini, and proposes served-safe promotion. So the owned model can climb to the D11 ship floor.

## Privacy: two independent guards keep it INERT in production today
1. `flywheel.enabled = False` (default) → the job-completion hook is a strict no-op.
2. `flywheel.consent_attested()` is a **FAKED HARD-DENY** — returns `False` for *every* job, **ignoring any consent-looking field** — so even `enabled=True` captures **nothing** until the real consent schema lands (counsel ToS/DPA → the consent columns → wiring).

The guardrail (D10 / CLAUDE.md): adults-only, explicit opt-in, train-on-derived + auto-delete-raw, **minors excluded**, paid tier never trains. A guardrail-**lock** test asserts the unpatched gate denies even a job that *claims* consent — so it can't be quietly relaxed without counsel + the migration.

## What's in it
- `backend/flywheel.py` — `capture_job` (→ per-video `VideoWorkspace` via `backend.storage.put`), `shadow_eval` (→ `eval.experiment.compare`/`compare_unlabeled`), `propose_promotion` (→ `eval.promotion.promote_if_served_safe`, **Gemini=control / owned=proposal**), `run_for_job` (gated orchestrator).
- `FlywheelConfig` (default-OFF) on `AppConfig`.
- A gated `_maybe_run_flywheel` job-completion hook mirroring M8's `_maybe_record_job_cost` (best-effort, never wedges a completed job).
- **No DB migration** — the skeleton persists no consent. (The future M9-consent migration is **v9**: v7=`locale`, v8=compute-picker.)

It's **plumbing only** — it reuses the real workspace/eval/promotion/storage code so the loop works the day consent + the owned model + golden labels are wired. No live behavior change.

## Verification
- `python run_all_tests.py` → **1457 passed, 7 skipped** (rebased on `origin/master` incl. the compute-picker #6d3d054). `ruff` + `mypy` clean.
- **+14 tests**: default-OFF no-op, enabled-but-consent-denied no-op, consented-capture writes the manifest, the guardrail-lock, shadow-eval/promote delegation (+ arg order), the hook is best-effort.
- Adversarial 2-lens review (`wf_1ee5ffdf`) → **ship**, `inert_guarantee=yes`; folded the guardrail-lock test.

⛔ LIVE activation stays owner/counsel (the consent ToS/DPA + schema) + owner/GPU (the owned model + golden corpus). Built in an isolated worktree off `origin/master`. Tracker + changelog updated.

48	def parse_uri(uri: str) -> StorageRef:
49	    """Parse a storage URI (or a bare filesystem path) into a ``StorageRef``.
50	
51	    A string with no ``scheme://`` prefix (including Windows ``C:\\...`` paths, which
52	    have no ``//``) is treated as a local filesystem path — preserving today's behaviour.
53	    """
54	    s = str(uri)
55	    m = _SCHEME_RE.match(s)
56	    if not m:
57	        return StorageRef("local", "", s, f"local://{s}")
58	    scheme = m.group(1).lower()
59	    rest = s[m.end():]
60	    if scheme == "gs":
61	        scheme = "gcs"
62	    if scheme in ("local", "gdrive"):
63	        # Path-keyed, no bucket: local FS, or a path under the mounted Google Drive "My Drive".
64	        return StorageRef(scheme, "", rest, s)
65	    if scheme in _KNOWN:
66	        bucket, _, key = rest.partition("/")
67	        return StorageRef(scheme, bucket, key, s)
68	    raise ValueError(f"unsupported storage URI scheme: {scheme}:// (in {s!r})")
69	
70	
71	def resolve_input_uri(raw: str, *, input_backend: str = "local", input_root: str = "") -> str:
72	    """Resolve a user-supplied input reference into a storage URI (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M2).
73	
74	    Precedence (first match wins):
75	      1. An explicit ``scheme://`` on ``raw`` (``gs://``, ``s3://``, ``local://``, ``gdrive://``) → as-is.
76	      2. An ABSOLUTE local path (``/data/x.mp4``, ``C:\\…``, ``C:/…``) → as-is (always local).
77	      3. A bare/relative name + a non-empty ``input_root`` → joined under it
78	         (``"gs://bucket/videos"`` + ``"x.mp4"`` → ``"gs://bucket/videos/x.mp4"``).
79	      4. A bare/relative name + ``input_backend != "local"`` → prefixed (``"gcs"`` + ``"bucket/x"`` →
80	         ``"gcs://bucket/x"``).
81	      5. Otherwise → ``raw`` unchanged (a relative/bare LOCAL path — today's behaviour).
82	
83	    So the default (``input_backend="local"``, ``input_root=""``) is an identity passthrough for every
84	    path used today, preserving byte-for-byte behaviour.
85	    """
86	    s = str(raw)
87	    if _SCHEME_RE.match(s):           # 1. explicit scheme wins
88	        return s
89	    # 2. an anchored LOCAL path is always local, never joined. Detect this OS-INDEPENDENTLY —
90	    #    os.path.isabs is platform- AND Python-version-dependent (on Windows + py3.13 a POSIX-style
91	    #    "/data/x" is NOT absolute). Anchored == a leading "/" or "\\", or a Windows drive prefix "X:".
92	    if s.startswith(("/", "\\")) or (len(s) >= 2 and s[1] == ":" and s[0].isalpha()):
93	        return s
94	    if input_root:                    # 3. join a relative name under the configured root
95	        return input_root.rstrip("/") + "/" + s.lstrip("/")
96	    if input_backend and input_backend != "local":   # 4. prefix a bucket/key with the declared backend
97	        return f"{input_backend}://{s}"
98	    return s                          # 5. bare/relative local path — unchanged

1	"""Google Cloud Storage backend — first-class object buckets.
2	
3	Uses ``google-cloud-storage``, imported lazily so ``import backend.storage`` never pulls it;
4	install with the ``storage-gcs`` extra. Credentials come from Application Default Credentials
5	(``GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`` pointing at a service-account JSON, or workload identity) —
6	never from config. A ``client`` may be injected for tests.
7	"""
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	from datetime import timedelta
11	from typing import Any, Iterator, Optional
12	
13	from backend.storage.base import StorageBackend
14	from backend.storage.ref import StorageRef, StorageStat
15	
16	
17	class GCSStorage(StorageBackend):
18	    def __init__(self, *, client: Any = None, project: Optional[str] = None,
19	                 signed_url_ttl: int = 3600, **_ignored) -> None:
20	        self._ttl = signed_url_ttl
21	        if client is not None:
22	            self._client = client
23	            return
24	        from google.cloud import storage  # type: ignore  # lazy: only when a gcs:// URI is used
25	        self._client = storage.Client(project=project)
26	
27	    def _blob(self, ref: StorageRef):
28	        return self._client.bucket(ref.bucket).blob(ref.key)
29	
30	    def fetch_to_local(self, ref, dst=None, *, overwrite=False) -> str:
31	        if dst is None:
32	            raise ValueError("GCSStorage.fetch_to_local requires a dst path")
33	        import os
34	        dst = str(dst)
35	        if os.path.exists(dst) and not overwrite:
36	            return dst
37	        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dst) or ".", exist_ok=True)
38	        self._blob(ref).download_to_filename(dst)
39	        return dst
40	
41	    def put(self, local_path, ref, *, content_type=None) -> StorageRef:
42	        self._blob(ref).upload_from_filename(str(local_path), content_type=content_type)
43	        return ref
44	
45	    def exists(self, ref) -> bool:
46	        return bool(self._blob(ref).exists())
47	
48	    def stat(self, ref) -> StorageStat:
49	        b = self._blob(ref)
50	        b.reload()
51	        return StorageStat(size=int(b.size or 0), modified=b.updated, etag=b.etag,
52	                           content_type=b.content_type)
53	
54	    def list(self, prefix) -> Iterator[StorageRef]:
55	        for blob in self._client.list_blobs(prefix.bucket, prefix=prefix.key):
56	            yield StorageRef("gcs", prefix.bucket, blob.name, f"gcs://{prefix.bucket}/{blob.name}")
57	
58	    def delete(self, ref) -> None:
59	        self._blob(ref).delete()
60	
61	    def signed_url(self, ref, *, ttl=3600, method="GET") -> str:
62	        return self._blob(ref).generate_signed_url(
63	            version="v4", expiration=timedelta(seconds=ttl or self._ttl), method=method)

1	"""The ``StorageBackend`` ABC — one interface for local + object-store + Drive.
2	
3	Mirrors the codebase's ABC+Registry idiom (cf. ``SegmenterRegistry``). The bucket backends
4	live in sibling modules and lazy-import their cloud SDK so ``import backend.storage`` never
5	pulls ``boto3`` / ``google-cloud-storage`` (``gdrive`` is pure local-FS over a mounted Drive —
6	no SDK). See ``PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md`` §3.1 and ``docs/DECOUPLED_COMPUTE/``.
7	"""
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	import os
11	from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
12	from typing import Iterator
13	
14	from backend.storage.ref import StorageRef, StorageStat
15	
16	
17	class StorageBackend(ABC):
18	    """Backend-agnostic object storage. ``fetch_to_local`` + ``put`` are the two the
19	    worker dispatcher needs; the rest support listing/checkpointing/serving."""
20	
21	    @abstractmethod
22	    def fetch_to_local(self, ref: StorageRef, dst: "str | os.PathLike[str] | None" = None,
23	                       *, overwrite: bool = False) -> str:
24	        """Make the object available as a local file; return its local path. For the
25	        ``local`` backend this is an identity passthrough (the object is already local)."""
26	
27	    @abstractmethod
28	    def put(self, local_path: "str | os.PathLike[str]", ref: StorageRef, *,
29	            content_type: "str | None" = None) -> StorageRef:
30	        """Upload/copy a local file to ``ref``; return ``ref``."""
31	
32	    @abstractmethod
33	    def exists(self, ref: StorageRef) -> bool: ...
34	
35	    @abstractmethod
36	    def stat(self, ref: StorageRef) -> StorageStat: ...
37	
38	    @abstractmethod
39	    def list(self, prefix: StorageRef) -> Iterator[StorageRef]:
40	        """Yield a ``StorageRef`` per object under ``prefix``."""
41	
42	    @abstractmethod
43	    def delete(self, ref: StorageRef) -> None: ...
44	
45	    def signed_url(self, ref: StorageRef, *, ttl: int = 3600, method: str = "GET") -> str:
46	        """A time-limited URL handing the bytes to a browser/LLM. Not every backend can

1	"""Google Drive backend — reads Drive files through a **locally mounted** Google Drive.
2	
3	We make a deliberate, baked-in assumption: **to use Google Drive files you mount Google Drive
4	for Desktop**, which exposes Drive as an ordinary filesystem path. That's a fair requirement — it
5	needs no service account, no API quota, no OAuth, and Drive streams + caches bytes on access. So
6	``gdrive://`` is just a *portable, OS-agnostic alias* over that mount: it names a path **relative to
7	your "My Drive" folder**, and everything else is plain local-filesystem I/O (the same code the
8	``local`` backend uses).
9	
10	    gdrive://Sharing/clip.mp4   ->   <mount>/My Drive/Sharing/clip.mp4
11	
12	Why an alias instead of just a raw path? Portability: the mount root differs per machine
13	(Windows ``G:\\My Drive``, macOS ``~/Library/CloudStorage/GoogleDrive-<acct>/My Drive``), so the same
14	``gdrive://`` URI resolves on any box with Drive mounted. The root is **auto-detected**, or set it
15	explicitly via ``GDRIVE_MOUNT_ROOT`` / ``storage.gdrive.mount_root`` (a path, not a secret).
16	
17	A headless box (a GPU VM, CI) has **no** mount — there, stage the file to a bucket and use
18	``gcs://`` / ``s3://``, or ``gdown <file-id>`` to a local path. This backend intentionally does NOT
19	talk to the Drive API. When the mount (or a file in it) is missing, errors point the user at the
20	fix: install Drive for Desktop and include the folder in syncing so it can be pulled locally.
21	"""

94	def get_storage(backend: Optional[str] = None, *,
95	                config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None, **overrides) -> StorageBackend:
96	    """Construct a backend. ``backend`` (or ``config['backend']``, default 'local') selects
97	    the implementation; its per-backend settings come from ``config[backend]`` merged with
98	    ``overrides``. Settings carry NO secrets — only endpoints/regions/paths/source-selectors."""
99	    cfg = config or {}

102	    settings.update(overrides)
103	    return _backend_class(name)(**settings)

gh pr create --base master --head serving/m12-gdrive-api --title "feat(serving): M12 — gdrive-api:// OAuth/API StorageBackend (D14 Northstar)" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## What & why
The **M12** Drive **API** StorageBackend — the headless cloud-native round-trip: read a source from + write the stitched reel **back to** a user's Google Drive via the Drive API (no local mount). The cloud counterpart to the mount-only `gdrive://` backend (`GDriveStorage`, **left untouched**). Slots into the `StorageBackend` ABC (D14 Northstar).

## What's in it
- **`backend/storage/gdrive_api.py`** — `GDriveApiStorage`. Path-keyed `gdrive-api://<path>` → each component resolved to a Drive file id via `files().list`. Implements `fetch_to_local` (`get_media().execute()` bytes), an **idempotent** `put` (delete-then-create so a re-put overwrites, never orphans a duplicate), `exists` / `stat` / `list` (paginated) / `delete`.
- **Scheme wiring**: `ref.py` (`_KNOWN` + the path-keyed branch — `_SCHEME_RE` already allowed the hyphen), `__init__._backend_class` (lazy import) + a **hyphen→underscore** settings lookup in `get_storage`, and `StorageConfig.backend`/`input_backend` Literals + a `gdrive_api` settings dict.
- **Lazy + injectable**: all Google libs are imported only inside `_build_service`/`_media_body` — `import backend.storage` never pulls `googleapiclient`/`google.auth` (asserted in a test). A `service` is injectable → fully-offline tests against an in-memory fake Drive.
- **No secrets in config** (creds via ADC over the Drive scope); `_build_service` raises a clear `storage-gdrive-api`-extra error when libs/creds are absent. **No DB migration.**

## Verification
- `python run_all_tests.py` → **1467 passed, 7 skipped**. `ruff` + `mypy` clean.
- **+10 hermetic tests**: scheme parse, registry + underscore-settings wiring, full round-trip, **idempotent re-put** (no duplicate), missing/dst guards, the ADC-build error.
- Adversarial 2-lens review (`wf_ae8c9b0f`): **`no_regression=yes`** (existing backends + defaults byte-identical); folded the **idempotent-put** fix (was: re-put orphaned duplicates) + the binary-files-only / first-match-on-duplicate caveats.

⛔ The **live per-user interactive OAuth app** (consumer "connect your Drive": client id/secret + refresh tokens) is **owner-gated**; this ships the service-account/ADC build + the fake. Built in an isolated worktree off `origin/master`. Tracker + changelog updated.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -3
[/external_agent_tool_call]

=== DEPLOY_ALPHA.md §1 prereqs + §2 engine (the box-side steps) ===
## 1. Prerequisites

- A **box** to run the engine: the CPU droplet `168.144.126.176` (Gemini-only — no GPU; fine for alpha) **or** your Windows+WSL GPU box (real WASB). `ffmpeg` + Python 3.8+ installed; the `badminton-highlight-indexer` engine checked out.
- A **Cloudflare account** with the `khelsutra.guru` zone (you already run the marketing site there).
- `cloudflared` installed on the box (`https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/connections/connect-networks/downloads/`).
- A **fresh** `GEMINI_API_KEY` (rotate the chat-shared one — it uploads downsized chunks to Google; this is a paid + cloud action).

---

## 2. Engine (the `api.` side)

### 2.1 Install (with the auth extra)
```bash
cd badminton-highlight-indexer
pip install -e .[auth]          # PyJWT[crypto] — REQUIRED when edge auth is on, else every authenticated request is rejected 401
```

### 2.2 The safe-exposed config
Create / edit `config.json` on the box. **Where it must live:** the engine reads `$RALLY_APP_HOME/config.json` if `RALLY_APP_HOME` is set, else `./config.json` relative to *the directory you launch the server from* — **not** necessarily the repo root. If the engine reads a different (or absent) file it silently falls back to all-defaults, which means **edge auth is OFF**. To make writer and reader agree by construction, write it with `python -m backend.main --setup` (it targets the exact path the server reads) and edit *that* file:
```jsonc
{
  "security": {
    "edge_auth_enabled": true,                       // turn the CF-Access JWT verify ON
    "allowed_video_root": "/srv/khelsutra/incoming",  // the path-jail — REQUIRED when exposed
    "upload_dir": "/srv/khelsutra/incoming/uploads",  // MUST sit UNDER allowed_video_root (see §7)
    "cf_team_domain": "https://<your-team>.cloudflareaccess.com",
    "cf_access_aud": "<the CF Access application AUD>"  // filled in §4
  },
  "indexing": {
    "default_segmenter": "gemini"  // ⚠ CPU box MUST set this — the shipped default `yolo_hybrid` needs torch/GPU, and the SPA sends no segmenter, so every job fails "No module named 'torch'" without it
  }
}
```
And export the env (a `.env` or your shell):
```bash
export GEMINI_API_KEY="<your-rotated-key>"
export RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS="https://app.khelsutra.guru"   # lock CORS to the SPA origin
export RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS="api.khelsutra.guru,localhost,127.0.0.1"  # REQUIRED — see ⚠ below; without it EVERY tunneled request 400s (loopback added so on-box /api/health checks still pass — setting this var REPLACES the loopback-only default)
export RALLY_BIND_HOST="127.0.0.1"                             # keep the loopback bind (cloudflared dials 127.0.0.1:8000)
```

> **⚠ Two env vars you MUST set, or the tunnel is dead-on-arrival.** With `edge_auth_enabled: true`, the engine (a) flips its bind to `0.0.0.0` and (b) keeps the DNS-rebinding **Host-header guard** at its loopback-only default — so it boots "healthy" but then **`400`s every request `cloudflared` forwards with `Host: api.khelsutra.guru`** (including the §6 `/api/health` check). The boot only *warns* about this (`EXPOSED_HOST_ALLOWLIST_LOOPBACK`), it does not stop. Set **`RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS="api.khelsutra.guru,localhost,127.0.0.1"`** (the load-bearing fix — the Host allowlist is independent of the bind; the loopback entries keep the on-box `/api/health` check passing, since setting this var REPLACES the loopback-only default) and **`RALLY_BIND_HOST="127.0.0.1"`** (so the bind stays loopback as §0/§6 claim; `cloudflared` dials loopback anyway). *V...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

# Droplet quickstart — KhelSutra alpha (the `api.` engine side)

The **filled-in, command-dense** version of `../DEPLOY_ALPHA.md` for the Linux CPU droplet
(`168.144.126.176`, Gemini, **no GPU**). Values are pre-baked for **`app.khelsutra.guru` /
`api.khelsutra.guru`**, team domain **`https://khelsutra.cloudflareaccess.com`**, allowlist
**`avi.dullu@gmail.com`**, jail **`/srv/khelsutra/incoming`**. Read `../DEPLOY_ALPHA.md` for the *why*;
this is the *what to type*. Files here:

| File | Goes to | Purpose |
|------|---------|---------|
| `config.json.example` | `/srv/khelsutra/config.json` | the `security` block (edge-auth + jail + CF team/AUD) |
| `engine.env.example` | `/etc/khelsutra/engine.env` | secrets + the **two `RALLY_*` exposure vars** + app-home |
| `khelsutra-engine.service` | `/etc/systemd/system/` | keeps the engine up (binds `127.0.0.1:8000`) |
| `../cloudflared.config.example.yml` | `/etc/cloudflared/config.yml` | the tunnel ingress (`api.` → `127.0.0.1:8000`) |

> **Cost reality:** Cloudflare Pages + Tunnel + Access are **free** (Access ≤ 50 users). **No $25/Pro
> plan.** When you enable Zero Trust you may be asked to add a card and "subscribe" — pick the **Free**
> plan; you won't be charged under 50 users. Standing cost ≈ $0 + Gemini per run.

---

## A. Engine on the box (one-time)

```bash
# 1. user + jail dirs (upload_dir MUST sit under allowed_video_root)
sudo useradd --system --create-home --home-dir /opt/khelsutra khelsutra || true
sudo mkdir -p /srv/khelsutra/incoming/uploads
sudo chown -R khelsutra:khelsutra /srv/khelsutra /opt/khelsutra

# 2. engine code + venv + the AUTH extra (REQUIRED with edge auth on, else every authed request 401s)
sudo -u khelsutra git clone <ENGINE_REPO_URL> /opt/khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer
sudo -u khelsutra python3 -m venv /opt/khelsutra/venv
sudo -u khelsutra /opt/khelsutra/venv/bin/pip install -e "/opt/khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer[auth]"
#    (ffmpeg is required by the engine: `sudo apt-get install -y ffmpeg`)

# 3. env file (fill GEMINI_API_KEY) — keep it 600
sudo mkdir -p /etc/khelsutra
sudo cp deploy/droplet/engine.env.example /etc/khelsutra/engine.env
sudo nano /etc/khelsutra/engine.env          # paste the rotated GEMINI_API_KEY
sudo chmod 600 /etc/khelsutra/engine.env

# 4. config.json at the app-home path the server actually reads ($RALLY_APP_HOME/config.json)
sudo -u khelsutra cp deploy/droplet/config.json.example /srv/khelsutra/config.json
#    cf_access_aud is still a placeholder — you fill it in step C after creating the Access app.

# 5. service
sudo cp deploy/droplet/khelsutra-engine.service /etc/systemd/system/
#    ^ edit WorkingDirectory + ExecStart paths if you didn't use /opt/khelsutra/...
sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl enable --now khelsutra-engine
journalctl -u khelsutra-engine -e            # expect a clean boot; a WARN about cf_access_aud is fine until step C
```

The boot **fails fast** (`exit 1`, `[STARTUP] refusing to start`) if `edge_auth_enabled=true` but the
jail or `cf_team_domain`/`cf_access_aud` is unset — that's your signal the config is read & wrong, not ignored.
If it boots silently claiming auth-on, the engine read a **different** `config.json` (check `RALLY_APP_HOME`).

## B. The tunnel (`api.khelsutra.guru` → the box)

```bash
# install cloudflared (Cloudflare apt repo), then:
cloudflared tunnel login                                  # browser OAuth to the khelsutra.guru zone
cloudflared tunnel create khelsutra-studio                # note the <TUNNEL_UUID> it prints
sudo mkdir -p /etc/cloudflared
sudo cp deploy/cloudflared.config.example.yml /etc/cloudflared/config.yml
sudo nano /etc/cloudflared/config.yml                     # put <TUNNEL_UUID> in BOTH lines (tunnel: + credentials-file:)
sudo cp ~/.cloudflared/<TUNNEL_UUID>.json /etc/cloudflared/   # move the creds where config.yml points
cloudflared tunnel route dns khelsutra-studio api.khelsutra.guru   # creates...

## Step 2 — the real CUJ (you, browser)

### 666. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.748Z)

Pairing caught a real blocker before you hit it. Here's the verdict:

**✅ The deploy is genuinely live and healthy** (nothing to install — cloudflared's already there):
- **Tunnel is connected + routing real traffic** — 3 QUIC connections (bom11/blr02/bom09), ingress correct (`app.` + `api.` → `127.0.0.1:8000`), and the log even shows a real `api.khelsutra.guru/api/compile` request hitting the engine earlier today.

**❌ The one blocker — the Gemini key is invalid** (this is exactly the silent "0 rallies" trap):
```
GET …/v1beta/models?key=AQ.Ab8…  →  HTTP 401  UNAUTHENTICATED
"Expected OAuth 2 access token, login cookie or other valid authentication credential"
```
The stored value (`AQ.Ab…`, len 53) is **not a Gemini API key** — it looks like an OAuth access token (`gcloud`-style). A real Gemini API key starts with **`AIza`** (~39 chars). The engine calls Gemini the same way, so every job would finish `done` with **0 rallies** and the error would only land in the log. Good thing we checked before sharing.

### The fix (needs you — I can't mint a key)
1. Create a real key at **https://aistudio.google.com/apikey** (a fresh one; revoke the `AQ.` one). It'll start with `AIza`.
2. Then either:
   - **You set it** (cleanest — keeps the secret out of this chat):

### 717. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.751Z)

**Found it.** `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=api.khelsutra.guru,localhost,127.0.0.1` — it's **missing `app.khelsutra.guru`**. The engine's DNS-rebinding Host-guard accepts `api.` (200) but **rejects `app.` with 400 "Invalid host header"**. So once you're past the Access login, the SPA request through the tunnel gets killed by the engine. (The public 302s are just the pre-auth gate; the failure is on the post-auth leg.)

Before I fix it, I need to know the *intended* topology — does the engine serve the SPA at `/` (single-origin, so the fix is just adding `app.` to the allowlist), or is `app.` supposed to be a separate Cloudflare Pages site? Let me check what `/` serves and where `app.` DNS points:

### 720. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.751Z)

**Root cause confirmed — and it's a clean fix.** The engine serves the **KhelSutra Studio SPA at `/`** (the bundled Vite app) and the API at `/api` — so this is a **single-origin** deploy: both `app.` and `api.` go through the tunnel to the engine. But `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` only lists `api.` (the runbook's split-topology value), so the engine's Host-guard **400s every `app.` request**. The SPA literally can't load through the tunnel. Adding `app.` to the allowlist fixes it. Applying now:

### 721. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.751Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Add app. to RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS + restart + verify both hosts serve
command: ssh -i /c/Users/avidu/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet -o BatchMode=yes root@168.144.126.176 'bash -s' <<'EOF'
echo "=== before ==="; grep '^RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=' /etc/khelsutra/engine.env
# add app.khelsutra.guru to the allowlist (idempotent: only if missing)
grep -q 'app.khelsutra.guru' /etc/khelsutra/engine.env || \
  sed -i 's#^RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=.*#RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=app.khelsutra.guru,api.khelsutra.guru,localhost,127.0.0.1#' /etc/khelsutra/engine.env
echo "=== after ==="; grep '^RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=' /etc/khelsutra/engine.env
systemctl restart khelsutra-engine
# wait for the engine to come back (remote loop, not a local sleep)

echo "engine: $(systemctl is-active khelsutra-engine)"
EOF

### 729. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.751Z)

All healthy now — public edge `302` (gate up), fast (0.07s), engine clean, allowlist persisted. **The 522 is resolved.**

## What was wrong + the fix
Your deploy is **single-origin** — the engine serves the **KhelSutra Studio SPA** at `/` *and* the API at `/api`, with both `app.` and `api.` routed through the one tunnel. But `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` only listed `api.` (it was copied from the runbook's *split*-topology value, where `app.` would've been on Pages). So the engine's DNS-rebinding Host-guard was **400-ing every `app.` request** — the SPA couldn't load post-login. The 522 itself was a transient origin-unreachable blip (during one of my restarts); the real wall was that 400. **Fixed:** added `app.khelsutra.guru` to the allowlist → restarted → `app.` now serves the SPA (200), persisted to `engine.env`.

## Your input question
- **Local upload → YES.** That's the alpha path: the SPA uploads your file (chunked) into the jail, then processes. Use this.
- **`gs://` link → NO** on this box. With the `allowed_video_root` jail on (tenant isolation), `/api/process` rejects non-local URIs by design (400) — so a bucket URI won't work through the gated SPA.
- **Google Drive `https://` share link → NO.** The engine doesn't fetch arbitrary web URLs; supported schemes are local paths (uploads), `gs://`/`s3://`, and `gdrive://` (mount). A Drive web link isn't one. (The new `gdrive-api://` backend I just built is the future for this, but it's not wired into the hosted input yet + needs the OAuth app.)

**Hard-refresh** https://app.khelsutra.guru (Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+R to clear the cached 522) → CF Access login (OTP to your email) → the Studio SPA loads → **upload a short clip** → Process → pick → Build → download.

### 741. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.752Z)

Post-auth 522 (after SSO) but `api.` works — that's the tell. A **522 means Cloudflare tried to reach the origin and the TCP connection timed out**, which in a tunnel setup means the request *isn't going through the tunnel at all*. Most likely: `api.`'s DNS is the tunnel CNAME (works), but **`app.`'s DNS record isn't routed to the tunnel** (it's pointing at the droplet's public IP / a stale record), so CF tries to hit the box directly on 443 → the engine's only on loopback → timeout → 522.

The tunnel *ingress* has `app.` (I saw it), but the **DNS route** for `app.` is the missing piece. Let me verify and fix it:

echo "=== cert.pem (needed for 'tunnel route dns' DNS API) — where? ==="

echo "=== can we list the tunnel + its current DNS routes? ==="

if [ -n "$CERT" ]; then
  TUNNEL_ORIGIN_CERT="$CERT" cloudflared tunnel list 2>&1 | head -6
  echo "--- routes for the tunnel ---"

fi

### 747. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.752Z)

Root cause pinned. The tunnel is remotely-managed, **tunnel ID `55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac`**. `api.` works because its DNS routes into the tunnel; **`app.`'s DNS record is *not* pointed at the tunnel** (it's resolving to the box/origin directly), so Cloudflare tries to reach the droplet on :443 — where nothing's listening (the engine is loopback-only) — → **522**. The tunnel's *ingress* has `app.` (I saw it), but the *DNS record* is the missing/wrong half. This is a Cloudflare-side fix I can't make from the box (no zone-DNS credential there).

## The fix (CF dashboard — ~1 min)

**Cloudflare dashboard → `khelsutra.guru` zone → DNS → Records.** Look at the `app` record and compare it to `api`:
- `api` is a **CNAME → `55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac.cfargotunnel.com`** (Proxied / orange cloud). That's why it works.
- `app` is almost certainly something else (a proxied **A** record to the droplet IP, or a stale record). 

**Make `app` match `api`:** edit/replace the `app` record to be a **CNAME**, target **`55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac.cfargotunnel.com`**, **Proxied (orange cloud ON)**. If Cloudflare won't let you (a conflicting record exists), **delete the old `app` record first**, then add the CNAME.

*(Equivalent path: Zero Trust → Networks → Tunnels → your tunnel → Public Hostnames — confirm `app.khelsutra.guru` is listed pointing at `http://127.0.0.1:8000`; if a conflicting DNS record is flagged, removing it lets the tunnel manage `app`'s DNS automatically.)*

Once saved, give it ~30s and reload `app.khelsutra.guru` (the Edge window) — it'll route through the tunnel to the engine and the SPA will load.

**Alternatively**, if you'd rather I do it: create a Cloudflare **API token** scoped to *Zone → DNS → Edit* for `khelsutra.guru` and paste it here — I'll fix + verify the record via the API in one shot (⚠ it'd be in this transcript, so rotate it after). The dashboard route avoids that.

Tell me once you've changed it and I'll confirm `app.` is routing correctly (and the log capture is still running for your upload run).

### 749. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.753Z)

The screenshot is the key — thank you. Now I can see exactly what's wrong:

- `api.khelsutra.guru` → **Tunnel → `khelsutra-studio`** (Proxied) ✓ — routes to the engine, works.
- `app.khelsutra.guru` → **CNAME → `khelsutra.pages.dev`** (Proxied) ← **this is the problem.** `app.` points to a **Cloudflare Pages** project, *not* the tunnel. That Pages project isn't serving (hence the 522 after auth) — and meanwhile the engine, which already serves a working SPA, never gets the `app.` request.

So my instinct was right (app. isn't on the tunnel) but the specifics differ: it's pointing at Pages, not the origin IP. The fastest, most reliable fix is to **point `app.` at the tunnel too** (single-origin — the engine already serves the SPA, and we proved `api.`/the tunnel works). - `api.khelsutra.guru` = **Tunnel → khelsutra-studio** → reaches the engine. Works.
- `app.khelsutra.guru` = **CNAME → khelsutra.pages.dev** → points at a **Cloudflare Pages** project that isn't serving (→ the 522 after login). The engine — which *already* serves a working SPA — never sees the `app.` request.

And I just confirmed the clincher: **the engine's bundled SPA calls the API same-origin (`/api`)** — there's no `api.khelsutra.guru` baked into the bundle. So if `app.` routes to the tunnel/engine, the SPA loads *and* its API calls hit the same engine — **no CORS, no preflight, nothing else to configure.** The engine already has `app.` in its allowlist (I set that) + a valid key. So this one DNS change makes the whole flow work.

## The exact change to make — repoint `app.` to the tunnel (same as `api.`)

**Simplest (one edit):** DNS → click **Edit** on the `app.khelsutra.guru` row → change the record from
- ❌ `CNAME` → `khelsutra.pages.dev`
- ✅ `CNAME` → **`55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac.cfargotunnel.com`**  (keep **Proxied / orange cloud ON**)

Save. That's the tunnel's ID (`khelsutra-studio`), so `app.` now routes through the tunnel to the engine — identical to `api.`.

**Equivalent (the "blessed" way, matches how `api.` was made):** delete the `app → khelsutra.pages.dev` record, then **Zero Trust → Networks → Tunnels → khelsutra-studio → Public Hostnames** → add **`app.khelsutra.guru`** → Service **HTTP** `localhost:8000`. That auto-creates the correct Tunnel DNS record. (The tunnel's ingress already lists `app.`, so this just fixes the DNS half.)

Either way you end up with `app` and `api` both pointing at `khelsutra-studio`.

**Why this over fixing the Pages project:** Pages would be the "split" design (SPA on CDN), but that project is broken, and it'd then need a cross-origin API base + the CORS OPTIONS-bypass. Repointing to the tunnel reuses the engine SPA that already works for `api.` — fewer moving parts, zero CORS. (You can revisit Pages later for CDN benefits if you want.)

I can see it working end to end through the UI — this is the real CUJ:

```
SPA loaded (/, assets, /api/capabilities, /api/config → 200)
POST /api/upload/init → 200
PUT  part/0 … part/7  → 200   ← chunked upload (8 parts, ~3 min — it's a large file)
POST /api/upload/.../complete → 200
POST /api/process → 202 Accepted
"Running validations for …/uploads/GX010142_1080p.mp4"   ← validation passed
GET /api/jobs/1c624159… → 200 (polling every few sec — job running)
```

So: **upload ✓, process accepted ✓, job running** — validations passed and it's now into Gemini segmentation. No errors. The file is `GX010142_1080p.mp4` (a full-length clip), so Gemini will chunk it and **take several minutes** (each ~32 MB chunk uploads + processes; the smoke clip's 3 chunks took ~110s, so a long match is proportionally longer). The job-poll loop is healthy.

Two things to expect:
- **Rallies will appear** once segmentation finishes — I'll catch the count in the logs.
- **The Build step is the one to watch on a large file** — a long synchronous `/api/compile` can hit the Cloudflare **524** (the async-compile gap, engine #329). If Build 524s, the reel is usually still produced server-side; and your **small backup clip** is the clean way to demo the full download if the big one stalls on Build.

It's cooking. Keep the tab open; I'll keep watching and report the moment the rally count lands (or ping me when the UI shows rallies / you hit Build). Nice — your first real alpha run is going through.

### 798. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.756Z)

Grounded — and the owner's instinct is right on both counts. Current state (verified against `origin/master`):
- **Backend is already refresh-safe** ✅ — jobs run server-side, persisted in the SQLite `jobs` table; a client refresh doesn't touch the running job.
- **But the SPA loses the in-flight `job_id` on refresh** — `useIngestJob.ts` holds it only in poll-loop state (no `localStorage`/URL persistence), so a refresh drops you back to the ingest form even though the job keeps running.
- **And progress looks static** — the SPA reflects `job.progress` (a coarse free-text phase), but the Gemini segmenter never pushes per-chunk progress (it *logs* "Extracting Chunk 3/7" but doesn't call the job-progress hook), so the text doesn't move for minutes.

Let me check for an existing issue, then file it:

### 801. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.756Z)

No existing dup; `enhancement` label exists. Filing the feature request in `avidullu/khelsutra` (the SPA repo — that's where the UX + refresh-resume lives), with the engine dependency noted:

### 802. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.756Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Create the feature-request issue in khelsutra
command: cd /c/Users/avidu/Projects/khelsutra
gh issue create --label enhancement --title "Resilient processing UX: survive page refresh + continually-updating progress" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
**Context (from the first live alpha run on the droplet, 2026-06-22).** A real ~8.3-min upload took ~5+ min of Gemini segmentation (7 chunks of 90s). During that window the SPA shows a coarse, near-static "processing" phase, and a **page refresh drops the user back to the ingest form** even though the job keeps running server-side. Users naturally refresh when nothing visibly moves — and then think they've lost their work.

### Already fine (do NOT re-solve)
The **backend is refresh-safe**: jobs run server-side and are persisted in the SQLite `jobs` table (the async job worker); a client refresh does **not** kill or affect the running job.

### The gaps
1. **SPA loses the in-flight `job_id` on refresh.** `web/src/hooks/useIngestJob.ts` holds the active `job_id` only in the poll-loop's runtime state — there's no `localStorage`/URL persistence. On refresh the client can't reconnect to the still-running job → the user lands back on the ingest form. (On *done* it sets `?video=<id>`, but mid-job there's nothing to resume from.)
2. **Progress looks static for minutes.** The SPA reflects `job.progress` (a coarse free-text phase), but the Gemini segmenter *logs* per-chunk progress ("Extracting Chunk 3/7") and never pushes it to the job-progress hook — so the phase text doesn't move during the multi-minute segmentation.

### Requirements / acceptance
- [ ] **Survive refresh** — persist the active `job_id` (URL `?job=<id>` and/or `localStorage`); on load, if a job is in-flight, **resume polling it** and reconnect to live progress, then hand off to the existing `?video=` flow on done. A refresh during processing must seamlessly re-attach to the running job, never lose it.
- [ ] **Continually-updating, reassuring progress** — a heartbeat / elapsed-time indicator + an animated state so the user always sees movement even when the engine phase is unchanged. Copy along the lines of *"This can take a few minutes — it's safe to leave or refresh this tab; you won't lose your place."*
- [ ] **(Engine, linked — repo `Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer`)** Report granular progress from the Gemini segmenter via the job-progress hook (e.g. `segmenting chunk 3/7`) so the SPA has real movement to show. Small change in `backend/pipeline/segmenters/gemini.py`.

### Nice-to-haves
- Show partial results as chunks complete.
- A Cancel button.
- Surface the active/recent job in history so a returning user can find it.

_Surfaced live while watching the first real alpha run — a long Gemini job + a refresh-fragile client is the most likely way an early tester gets confused or "loses" a working job._
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2
[/external_agent_tool_call]

### 807. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.757Z)

Filed the feature request — and here's the cost answer.

## ✅ Feature request filed
[avidullu/khelsutra#111](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/issues/111) — "Resilient processing UX: survive page refresh + continually-updating progress" (`enhancement`). It captures both halves accurately: backend is *already* refresh-safe (jobs are DB-durable), so the work is (a) persist+resume the `job_id` client-side across refresh, (b) a heartbeat/elapsed progress indicator, and (c) a linked engine tweak to push per-chunk progress.

## 💰 Cost for this run (estimated — the engine doesn't meter it yet)
Verified facts: the engine uses `gemini-flash-latest` at **default media resolution** (it doesn't set `media_resolution=low`), and this run sent **7 chunks × 90s ≈ 630s of video** to Gemini.

- Video input ≈ 630s × ~300 tokens/s ≈ **~190k input tokens**; output (rally JSON) is negligible.
- `gemini-flash-latest` currently resolves to a **Gemini 3 Flash** build (~$0.50/1M input, per the official changelog — high confidence): **≈ $0.10 for this ~8-min match** (~**$0.01 per minute of footage**). *(If the alias has since moved to the pricier 3.5-Flash GA at $1.50/1M, it'd be ~$0.30 — check AI Studio billing for the truth.)*
- **OR $0 if your key is on Gemini's free tier** — but ⚠ **free-tier traffic can be used by Google to train on**, whereas the **paid tier does not train on your data**. For an alpha handling users' footage, you likely want billing enabled (paid) for the privacy guarantee — it's only pennies. Check AI Studio → your key's project to see which tier you're on.

Fixed costs (droplet ~$24/mo, Cloudflare $0) are not per-run, so **per-run ≈ the Gemini cost above**.

**Two follow-ups worth noting:**
1. The **M8 cost-ledger is built to show this per-job in the UI**, but the Gemini `usageMetadata` (token counts) isn't wired into it yet (the documented M8 gap). Wiring that = exact per-run cost, automatically. Want me to file that issue?
2. Setting `media_resolution=low` (~100 tok/s) would **cut the Gemini cost ~3×** (~$0.03/match) for some accuracy tradeoff — a cheap config knob.

## ⏳ Your job
Still running (~10 min in) — no errors, poll loop healthy. The slowness is the CPU droplet chewing through **60fps 1080p** frame extraction across 7 chunks (the smoke clip was 30fps/3 chunks and finished in ~2 min). This is exactly why your **small backup clip** is the better demo of the full upload→reel→download, and why the per-match speed argues for the GPU/Cloud-Run path later. I'll report the rally count the moment it lands.

gh issue create --repo Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer --label bug --title "[cost] /api/process re-runs the paid segmenter for an already-processed video — refresh/\"Try again\" re-bills Gemini" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
**Severity: cost + correctness.** A page refresh or the SPA's "Try again" re-uploads the same file and POSTs `/api/process` again. Since `video_id` is the **MD5 of the file**, the re-submit carries the **same `video_id`** — but the engine **re-runs the full (paid) segmenter anyway** instead of returning the existing result. On the Gemini path that's a repeated, real API charge (≈ $0.10 per 8-min match) for zero new information, plus another multi-minute compute run.

### Confirmed current behavior (`backend/main.py`)
`_execute_processing` computes `was_reingest = db_instance.get_video(video_id) is not None` — i.e. it **detects** the video is already processed — but uses that only for destroy-after-confirm safety (`_finalize_reingest` prunes/replaces). There is **no "already processed → reuse, skip the segmenter" path**: it runs the segmenter and replaces the segments regardless. So a re-submit re-bills.

### Live trigger (alpha run, 2026-06-22)
An 8.3-min upload finished with 27 rallies, but the SPA's 10-min poll ceiling timed out **~seconds before completion** and showed "Couldn't process — Try again." Clicking "Try again" would have re-uploaded the identical file and re-run Gemini end-to-end. (We recovered by loading `?video=<id>` instead.) The SPA-side resume is tracked in `avidullu/khelsutra#111`; **this issue is the engine-side guardrail** so we're protected even if a re-submit slips through (defense in depth).

### Proposed fix
- Make `/api/process` **idempotent by content hash**: if `video_id` already has valid `play_segments` and no explicit `force`/`reprocess` flag, **return the existing result immediately** (the queryable `video_id`) **without invoking the paid segmenter**.
- Re-processing must be **opt-in** (`force=true` / an explicit "Re-run" action) so it's never accidental.
- Optional earlier short-circuit: if the upload hashes to an existing `video_id`, skip the re-upload too.

### Acceptance
- [ ] Re-submitting an already-processed video (same file/hash) returns the cached rallies with **zero** segmenter/Gemini calls (assert no Gemini call in a test).
- [ ] `force=true` still re-runs (explicit), preserving the existing re-ingest destroy-after-confirm safety.
- [ ] Default (no force) is a no-op-cost reuse.
- [ ] Pairs with `avidullu/khelsutra#111` (SPA resume so a refresh doesn't re-trigger in the first place).

**Cost context:** paid Gemini ≈ $0.10 per 8-min match; at alpha scale, accidental re-runs are real money + a nasty surprise on a metered key. The M8 cost-ledger exists but doesn't meter Gemini usage yet, so a silent re-bill wouldn't even show up.
EOF

### 840. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.759Z)

Checkpoint saved to memory (tonight's milestone + the two fixes + the two bugs + the next-phase plan). The priced comparison is researching now — but here's the **decision framing** so you can sleep on it; the numbers will fill in shortly:

**The shape of the call (independent of exact prices):**
- **Your workload is small.** WASB/TrackNet is a *tiny* CV model (well under 8 GB VRAM), and the owned-model corpus is ~6–15 matches. So you do **not** need an A100/H100 — that's the expensive trap. The real choice is **T4 (cheapest "real" GPU) vs L4 (already proven on the M7 run, better perf/$)**. I'll price both.
- **Two cost levers that matter more than the GPU choice:** (1) **Spot/preemptible** instances (~60–80% off — fine for batch testing/training where an interruption just means re-run), and (2) **spin-up → work → DELETE** (you only pay while it's up; an idle GPU VM is the money sink). With those, this phase is pennies-to-a-few-dollars, not a monthly bill.
- **Form factor:** a **GPU VM you SSH into** (via the existing `deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh` — auto Ops-Agent, zone-sweep) for interactive train/infer/harness work — *not* Cloud Run (that's for serving). Pull the corpus straight from `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus` so there's no local-box dependency.
- **GCP vs DO:** your ADR-013 already picked GCP (DO GPU isn't even enabled on your account), but I'm pulling DO/Paperspace numbers too so you can confirm the call with data rather than take it on faith.

So my recommendation will almost certainly be: **GCP L4 (or T4 if you want rock-bottom) on Spot, in Singapore (Mumbai L4 stocks out), spun up per-session and deleted after** — and the harness is already there to point at the bucket. I'll come back with the priced table (T4/L4/A100 × on-demand/Spot × region, + DO/Paperspace) and a concrete "tomorrow morning" runbook the moment the research lands. **Reported from live alpha use (2026-06-22):** clicking **Share to WhatsApp** on a finished reel opens WhatsApp with a **pre-filled text message but no video** — the user expects the reel itself to be shared.

### Current behavior
`web/src/components/CompileResult.tsx:79` builds a `https://wa.me/?text=<message>` link. As the in-code comment notes, `wa.me` can carry **text only** — it can't attach the local file — so the user is silently left to manually attach the just-downloaded reel. Most users read "Share" as "share the video," so this reads as broken.

### Why a *link* won't fix it either
The reel lives at `GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename}`, which is **behind Cloudflare Access** — a recipient outside the tenant can't open a shared link. So the fix is to share the **file**, not a URL.

### Proposed fix — Web Share API with the file
- Fetch the reel as a blob (the SPA holds the CF-Access session): `fetch(highlightUrl(...))` → `blob` → `new File([blob], build.filename, { type: "video/mp4" })`.
- If `navigator.canShare?.({ files })` → `navigator.share({ files: [file], title, text: t("result.shareText") })` — on supported platforms (mobile Chrome/Safari, capable desktops) this hands the **actual video** to WhatsApp and any other share target.
- **Graceful fallback** where file-share is unsupported (many desktops): keep a download + a clear hint ("Reel downloaded — attach it in WhatsApp"), or the current `wa.me` text link. Never a dead/empty share.

### Acceptance
- [ ] On a file-share-capable browser (mobile especially), Share hands the **reel video** to WhatsApp/other targets — not just text.
- [ ] On unsupported browsers, a graceful, clearly-explained fallback (download + "attach it" guidance).
- [ ] Localized share text (`result.shareText`) retained.

### Honest scope note
Desktop browsers — including **Edge, where this was hit** — have limited Web-Share-*file* support, so they may still fall back to download-then-attach. The real win is **mobile + capable browsers getting a true one-tap video share** (which is where most reels get shared anyway). A public/signed shareable reel URL (so a plain link works for anyone) is a separate, privacy-sensitive feature worth its own issue if you want link-sharing too.
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2
[/external_agent_tool_call]

### 869. user (2026-07-02T06:11:32.761Z)

### 870. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.761Z)

Quick one — it's a Cloudflare **Zero Trust** dashboard edit (not the DNS page):

1. **[one.dash.cloudflare.com](https://one.dash.cloudflare.com)** → **Access → Applications**
2. Open the application that covers **`app.khelsutra.guru`** (the same one gates `api.` too — one app, so one edit covers both) → **Edit**
3. **Policies** tab → open your existing **Allow** policy (the one with the *Emails* rule that currently has `avi.dullu@gmail.com`)
4. In the **Include → Emails** rule, **add the tester's email**(s) → **Save**

That's it — effective on their next visit.

**What the tester does:** goes to `app.khelsutra.guru` → enters their email → gets a **one-time PIN** in their inbox → enters it → they're in (no Cloudflare account needed; session lasts hours). GitHub SSO also works if they prefer, since that IdP's wired up.

**Two tips:**
- **For more than a handful of testers**, use **Access → Lists** (or "Emails ending in `@yourdomain`") instead of editing the policy each time — define the list once, reference it in the rule, then just add emails to the list. Cleaner at scale.
- **It's free up to 50 users**, so the whole alpha cohort costs nothing on the gate.

(I'd offer to add someone via the API, but the token you dropped is read-only — DNS-scoped — and Access-policy edits need an `Access: Edit` scope anyway, so the dashboard is genuinely the fastest path here.)

That's the last piece — your alpha's ready to share. 🏸
